### Image prompt

```text
REFERENCE IMAGE 1 is the exact shot to reproduce. Recreate it faithfully: the same young woman (same face, same long dark brown hair, same cream cable-knit headband, same black quilted puffer jacket, same black leggings, same tall tan sheepskin winter boots), the same pose bent forward at the waist with both hands pressing down on the T-handle of a tall black manual hand pump standing on the driveway, the same large partly-deflated inflatable Santa Claus decoration lying slumped and flat on the snowy concrete to her left with a black air hose running from it to the pump, the same white American suburban house behind her with the open double garage on the left showing a cluttered garage interior, the same thin dusting of snow on the driveway and lawn, the same flat overcast winter daylight.

CHANGE ONE THING ONLY: RAISE THE CAMERA so there is generous empty headroom at the top of the frame for a text overlay. The camera is held higher, at about standing eye level, tilted very slightly down. The roofline of the house drops to roughly one third of the way down the frame, and the TOP QUARTER OF THE FRAME IS CLEAN EMPTY OVERCAST SKY with nothing in it. The woman stays fully in frame from the crown of her head to her boots, positioned in the lower two thirds of the picture, with a wide clear gap of sky above her head. Everything else - her pose, her clothing, the inflatable Santa, the pump, the garage, the snow, the light - is unchanged from the reference.

Vertical 9:16 phone-video still. Extremely high quality, hyper realistic, crisp real-estate-photography clarity, evenly exposed, sharp focus front to back, fine natural texture in the snow, the jacket fabric and the vinyl of the inflatable. NOT cinematic, no colour grading, no vignette, no shallow depth of field, no bokeh, no haze, no bloom, no motion blur, no CGI polish. Real amateur phone photo.
```

### Video prompt

```text
Animate the reference image exactly as it is. The first frame of the video IS the reference image, unchanged — same snowy driveway, same open garage full of clutter, same white house with the 3217 number plate and coach lamp, same slumped red-and-green inflatable Santa lying collapsed face-up on the wet plowed driveway, same tall black hand pump with its black hose running to the Santa, same woman in the black puffer jacket, cream knitted headband, black leggings and tan fur-topped snow boots.

CAMERA — HELD IPHONE, LOCKED FOCAL LENGTH. A friend is standing on the driveway filming her on a phone in portrait. The framing never changes size: NO ZOOM IN, NO ZOOM OUT, NO PUSH IN, NO PULL BACK, NO DOLLY, NO CRANE, NO ORBIT. Handheld micro-shake only — a few pixels of organic drift, the kind a real hand makes. The house, the garage opening and the Santa stay exactly the same size in frame from the first frame to the last.

MOVEMENT — A CLEARLY DIFFERENT POSE EVERY HALF SECOND. Her body never rests for a single frame. Her FACE fully redraws between beats — eyebrows, eyes and mouth are completely different shapes from one beat to the next: effort, hope, doubt, disgust, fury. Big whole-body actions, not small gestures.

ACTION TIMELINE (5 seconds, one continuous take, no cuts):
0.0-0.6s — she drives the pump handle DOWN hard with both hands, shoulders dropping, elbows locking out, knees bending, breath fogging out of her mouth, jaw clenched.
0.6-1.2s — the handle rips back UP and her whole torso lifts with it, hair swinging, then slams DOWN again even harder. Two full fast strokes inside this window.
1.2-2.0s — third and fourth strokes, faster and more violent, her boots shifting for grip on the icy driveway, her weight thrown into each push. The Santa reacts: the red belly rounds out a little and the left arm lifts a few inches off the ground, the vinyl rippling and trembling. It inflates only a LITTLE. It never stands up, it never becomes full, it stays collapsed on the driveway for the entire video.
2.0-2.6s — she stops dead, straightens up, stares down at the pathetic half-lumpy Santa with her mouth open and her eyebrows slammed together, chest heaving. The Santa's arm sags straight back down and the belly deflates again with a visible wobble.
2.6-3.4s — she SHOVES the pump away from her with both hands and it topples over onto the snow; she throws both arms out sideways in a furious "are you kidding me" gesture and shouts, mouth wide open, head thrown forward.
3.4-4.2s — she takes one big step forward and KICKS the inflatable hard with her right boot — a full committed swing from the hip, arms flying up for balance, her whole body twisting through the kick. The vinyl caves in at the impact point and the whole Santa slides and flops sideways across the wet driveway, the hose whipping after it.
4.2-5.0s — she plants both feet, rakes the hair off her face, jabs a finger down at the Santa and screams again, red-faced, then throws both hands up and turns half away in disgust.

HARD RULES: the Santa NEVER fully inflates and NEVER stands upright — it only puffs slightly, then collapses again. The pump hose stays attached to the Santa the whole time. No other people enter the frame. The house, the garage contents and the parked items never change or move. NO TEXT, NO LETTERS, NO WORDS, NO NUMBERS, NO CAPTIONS, NO SUBTITLES, NO WATERMARK anywhere in the video.

AUDIO: natural room sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Cold outdoor winter ambience, the wheeze and thud of a hand pump, vinyl crinkling, boots scraping on wet ice, one furious wordless female shout, a dull thump on the kick.

LOOK: real iPhone UGC. Flat overcast winter daylight, slightly cool white balance, mild sensor noise in the shadows, natural skin, no cinematic grade, no lens flare, no slow motion, no speed ramp.
```

### Rejected takes

- **v20_c01_g1.png · take 1** — Camera at chest height, so the roofline sat near the top edge and there was nowhere clean to drop the hook text.
- **v20_c01_g2.png · take 2** — Camera dropped further and she folded right down over the pump — Santa reads well, but the frame is all house and no sky.

### What made it work

Regenerate by naming the single change, not by rewriting the prompt. The take-3 prompt is just take 1 fed back as @Image 1 plus “CHANGE ONE THING ONLY: RAISE THE CAMERA…” and an explicit description of the new space — “the TOP QUARTER OF THE FRAME IS CLEAN EMPTY OVERCAST SKY with nothing in it.” Naming the headroom as an object is what produced it; asking for “more headroom” does not.

A kick has to be written as a whole body, not a foot. “She kicks it” produces a nudge. What landed the beat is naming the chain — weight onto the left leg, the right boot swinging through from the hip, the vinyl caving in where it lands, the Santa sliding away and flopping, the hose whipping after it. Partial inflation must be stated as a limit twice. “It inflates only a LITTLE… it stays collapsed on the driveway for the entire video” — without the second clause the model stands the Santa fully up by the end and kills the joke.

### Video prompt

```text
TWO SCENES IN ONE CLIP, JOINED BY ONE HARD CUT AT EXACTLY 2.5s. No dissolve, no fade, no whip pan, no transition effect — a straight in-camera cut, exactly like a creator cutting two takes together.

REFERENCE IMAGES. Reference image 1 is the first frame of scene 1 — start there, unchanged. Reference image 2 is the CLOSED PRODUCT BOX: the artwork on the box in this video must match it exactly. Reference image 3 is the OPEN BOX INTERIOR: what she reveals in scene 2 must match it exactly.

BOX SPEC — TRUE IN EVERY FRAME. A matte black rigid retail box, 55 cm wide x 42 cm tall x 10 cm deep. It is BIG — much wider than the span of two outstretched hands; each of her hands covers less than a quarter of the box width, and laid on the sofa seat it spans most of one cushion. Front panel top to bottom: a large white condensed uppercase headline LED CURTAIN LIGHTS; a warm gold subline Create Your Own Magic; a red diagonal BEST SELLER ribbon flag in the top-right corner; one large photographic panel with a thin gold hairline border showing a night house facade with a glowing pixel LED Christmas tree on the garage door; a bottom row of five small gold line icons with white captions. NO transparent window, NO clear plastic panel, NO die-cut cutout, NO product visible through the front, NO glowing lights showing through the cardboard. The box is closed and sealed until the moment she opens it in scene 2.

SCENE 1 — 0.0 to 2.5s. CAMERA: HELD IPHONE, LOCKED FOCAL LENGTH, portrait, a friend filming from where the reference image was taken. NO ZOOM IN, NO ZOOM OUT, NO PUSH IN, NO DOLLY. Handheld micro-shake only. The living room behind her — stone fireplace with hanging stockings, the two lit Christmas trees, the lamp, the cream sofa, the wood floor — stays exactly as it is and never changes.
THE WHOLE POINT OF SCENE 1: she carries the box round in front of the sofa, sets it flat on the seat cushion AND drops to her knees IN THE SAME CONTINUOUS MOTION. These are not two separate steps — the box going down and her body going down are one move.
0.0-0.5s — she takes a step forward and to her right, turning her shoulders, swinging the heavy box out in front of her; her hair swings across, her cream fleece sleeve creases with the weight.
0.5-1.3s — she steps in front of the sofa, leans forward from the hips and lowers the box toward the empty seat cushion, arms extending, knees already bending underneath her.
1.3-1.9s — the box lands flat and printed-side-up on the cushion with a soft compression of the cushion, her hands still on it, and in the same motion her knees come down onto the wood floor, one after the other, her weight settling, her back rounding forward.
1.9-2.5s — kneeling now, she shuffles closer to the sofa, slides the box squarely in front of her with both hands, pushes her hair behind one ear and plants both palms flat on the box lid, chest rising with an excited breath.

HARD CUT AT 2.5s.

SCENE 2 — 2.5 to 5.0s. Direct continuation, same room, same second, same woman, same clothes, same box in exactly the same place on the same cushion. The camera has moved about 30 degrees round to her side — a slightly more side-on three-quarter angle, a little lower, closer to the level of the sofa seat, so we now see the lid edge in profile and read the pivot clearly. Still HELD IPHONE, LOCKED FOCAL LENGTH, handheld micro-shake only, NO ZOOM.
LID MECHANISM, GEOMETRICALLY. The box is a magnetic-closure flip-top. The hinge runs along the TOP LONG EDGE of the box — the far edge away from her, at the back. The lid pivots UP and BACK on that hinge, rotating away from her and toward the back of the sofa, keeping its printed front face turned toward the camera as it rises. The inside of the lid is matte black. The lid is NEVER lifted off, NEVER removed, NEVER carried aside, NEVER set down, NEVER folded flat instantly, NEVER slid out like a drawer.
2.5-3.0s — she hooks her fingertips under the near front edge of the lid; a tiny snap as the magnets release and the front edge lifts a centimetre, a thin black shadow line opening across the whole 55 cm width.
3.0-4.0s — she pivots the big printed lid UP and BACK on its hinge in one smooth continuous arc with real cardboard weight, her elbows rising, her shoulders lifting, until it stands up roughly vertical. It travels slowly and heavily — it does not flip open instantly.
4.0-4.6s — the interior is revealed exactly as reference image 3: the neatly folded black LED mesh curtain in its tray with the coiled lead and the small accessory items. She reaches in with both hands and touches the folded mesh, fingers spreading it slightly.

HARD RULES: exactly ONE cut, at 2.5s. The box never changes size, never changes artwork, never gains a window or a cutout. Nobody else enters frame. NO TEXT, NO LETTERS, NO WORDS, NO NUMBERS, NO CAPTIONS, NO SUBTITLES, NO WATERMARK anywhere in the video.

AUDIO: natural room sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Quiet warm living-room ambience, the soft thump of a heavy box on a cushion, knees on a wood floor, the click of magnets releasing, stiff cardboard flexing, plastic mesh rustling, one small delighted exhale.

LOOK: real iPhone UGC indoors at night. Warm mixed lamp and fairy light, slight sensor noise in the shadows, natural skin, no cinematic grade, no slow motion, no speed ramp.

### Rejected takes

- **v20_c02_g1.png · take 1** — Box came out small and held low against the sofa arm, and she is not cropped by the left edge. Composition was described in words instead of pinned to the screenshot.
- **v20_c02_g3.png · take 3** — Framing and box size fixed by the screenshot, but the seat cushions are buried under throw and plaid pillow — nowhere for the box to land in the next beat.

### What made it work

Three references, three jobs, each spelled out before any description starts. Composition from @Image 1, face from @Image 2, box art from @Image 3 — plus “ignore its room, its colours, its text overlay and its box artwork” so the framing reference cannot leak content. Empty space has to be described as a thing. “COMPLETELY EMPTY AND CLEAR — a long unbroken stretch of bare cushion with nothing resting on it: no boxes, no bags, no blankets” is what finally cleared the seat; “an empty sofa” never did.

A hard cut inside one clip works if you give it a timestamp and a reason to exist. “TWO SCENES IN ONE CLIP, JOINED BY ONE HARD CUT AT EXACTLY 2.5s” plus a described angle change for scene 2 (about 30° round to her side and lower) produced a clean single cut — scene detection finds it at 2.29s and nowhere else. Write the lid as a mechanism. Which edge hinges, which way it pivots, which face stays toward camera, what colour the inside is — then a ban list: never lifted off, never removed, never carried aside, never slid out like a drawer.

### Image prompt

```text
Two reference images. REFERENCE IMAGE 1 is the shot to reproduce - keep its framing, its camera angle, its woman, her face, her hair, her clothing, her pose, the black folding step stool, the light warm greige painted brick windowless wall, the white roof trim, the snow, the distant neighbouring house and bare trees at the right edge, and the flat overcast winter daylight. REFERENCE IMAGE 2 is a photo of the real product lying in its box and governs ONLY what the LED curtain is made of.

CHANGE EXACTLY ONE THING - SHE IS NOW HOLDING A THICK GATHERED CHUNK OF THE LED CURTAIN IN HER HANDS.

Her hands are still raised to the top of the wall in the same place and the same pose, but instead of pressing flat against the wall they are GRIPPING A FISTFUL OF THE NET ITSELF. Her far raised hand pinches the white header lead against the top of the wall; her near raised hand squeezes a thick bunched-up wad of the curtain - a dozen strands bundled and compressed together between her fingers, soft crumpled netting and thin dark wire visibly squashed in her grip, small white bulbs poking out of the bundle. It reads unmistakably as a gathered handful of the curtain, not an empty hand.

From that fistful a LARGE LOOSE MASS of the rest of the curtain hangs down: it drapes untidily down the wall below her hands, sags across in front of her body, and spills into a big tangled heap of netting that POOLS ON THE SNOWY GROUND at the lower right of the frame beside the step stool. Most of the curtain is still loose and unhung - only the section pinched in her hands is up at the top of the wall. The heap on the ground is soft and rumpled with visible loops of strand, bulbs scattered through it.

The curtain is built exactly like REFERENCE IMAGE 2: a WHITE insulated header lead cable along the top, thin dark vertical wires hanging from it side by side, each carrying small ROUND WHITE frosted bulbs spaced evenly along it. The white bulbs read clearly as bright white dots against the pale wall - dozens of them, individually countable, sharp and distinct. The horizontal gap between neighbouring strands is visibly WIDER than the vertical gap between bulbs on the same strand, so the grid is tall and rectangular, never a square matrix. The curtain is UNLIT and switched off - white plastic and glass catching the daylight, not glowing, emitting no light. Where it hangs against the wall it is completely SEE-THROUGH: between the strands you clearly read the pale brick behind, and the strands sag with an uneven wavy bottom hem. It is NOT an LED video wall, NOT a screen, NOT a panel, NOT a projection, NOT a solid sheet, NOT a fabric curtain.

Everything else stays exactly as in REFERENCE IMAGE 1: the same young woman with long dark brown hair in a cream quilted puffer jacket and black leggings, standing on the same small black folding step stool, seen from behind and slightly to her right, arms raised to the top of the wall; the light warm greige painted brick wall filling most of the frame with NO windows anywhere on it; snow on the ground; a neighbouring house and bare trees far off at the right edge; flat overcast winter daylight.

Vertical 9:16 phone-video still. Extremely high quality, hyper realistic, crisp real-estate-photography clarity, evenly exposed, sharp focus front to back, fine texture in the painted brick and the puffer jacket. NOT cinematic, no colour grading, no vignette, no shallow depth of field, no haze, no bloom, no motion blur, no CGI polish. Real amateur phone photo.

### Video prompt

```text
Animate the reference image exactly as it is. The first frame of the video IS the reference image, unchanged.

CAMERA - HELD IPHONE, LOCKED FOCAL LENGTH AND LOCKED FRAMING, low three-quarter angle from the driveway looking up the wall exactly as framed in the image. Handheld micro-shake ONLY - a few pixels of organic drift, nothing more. ABSOLUTELY NO ZOOM, no punch-in, no push-in, no pull-back, no dolly, no orbit, NO PAN LEFT OR RIGHT, NO TILT UP OR DOWN, no reframing, no speed ramp, no slow motion, no cuts. The wall, the gutter line and the step stool stay in exactly the same place in frame from the first frame to the last - if the wall slides across frame, it is wrong.

SCENE - LOCKED: the exact exterior in the image. Same long plain WINDOWLESS pale greige painted brick wall, same white gutter and roof edge running across the top of frame, same flat roofline, same flat overcast blue-grey winter daylight, same snow on the ground along the base of the wall, same clipped snow-dusted shrubs at the right edge. Same woman seen from behind, identical cream quilted puffer jacket, black leggings and white sneakers, identical long dark hair down her back, standing on the same black folding step stool. Same LED net already hung flat across the left two-thirds of the wall, same gathered fistful of unlit net in her raised hands, same loose curtain pooling down the wall and heaping on the snow beside the stool.

ACTION TIMELINE (4 seconds, one take):
0.0-1.3s - her left hand presses the dense unlit curtain mesh flat against the wall and holds it there; with her right hand she lifts the loose bundle of strands up and shakes it out so the strands unfurl and hang down the wall, swinging naturally and settling under their own weight.
1.3-2.6s - she reaches up along the top edge and presses the header wire into place under the gutter, running her hand sideways along the roofline, smoothing the mesh as she goes. Her weight shifts on the step stool, the puffer jacket creases, her body twists a little to reach.
2.6-4.0s - she steps down off the stool into the snow and takes one step back from the wall, STILL FACING THE WALL THE WHOLE TIME, and drops her arms to her sides. She tilts her head back to look up at the top of the curtain she has just hung. The back of her head and the back of her jacket stay toward the camera.

ABSOLUTE HARD RULE - SHE NEVER TURNS AROUND. Her face is NEVER visible at any point in the four seconds, not even for one frame, not even partly. She does not turn her head toward the camera, she does not turn her body toward the camera, she does not look over her shoulder, she does not glance back, there is NO profile view, NO three-quarter front view, NO cheek, NO nose, NO eye, NO chin visible. Her chest and face stay pointed at the wall for the entire clip. The camera stays behind her. When she steps down and steps back, she stays square to the wall with her back to camera.

PACE: real time, one continuous unhurried take. Every movement happens at normal human speed with natural weight behind it - she is working calmly, not rushing. NO fast motion, NO sped-up footage, NO time-lapse, NO jittery or twitchy limbs, NO frantic hand movement, NO extra actions squeezed in between the three beats above. Three unhurried actions across four seconds is the whole clip.

HARD RULES:
- The LED curtain stays COMPLETELY OFF and dark the entire time. Not one bulb lights up, no glow, no colour.
- Same wall, same house, same light throughout. The wall never gains windows, doors, vents or decoration.
- The curtain hangs as separate vertical strands of individual unlit bulbs, dense and evenly spaced, flat against the wall, obeying gravity, with an uneven wavy bottom hem. The loose coil stays resting in the snow on the ground with proper contact shadows - nothing floats.
- No cuts, no text, no captions, no graphics, no watermark.

AUDIO: natural room sound only - no music, no score, no background song. Cold outdoor ambience, faint wind, plastic strands rattling against brick, a step-stool creak, boots compressing snow. NO voice, NO narration, nobody speaks in this shot.

LOOK: real iPhone UGC in flat overcast daylight, natural noise, not cinematic, not graded, no slow motion.
```

### Rejected takes

- **v20_c03_g1.png · take 1** — Wall came out near-black. White bulbs would have vanished against it, so the whole install beat would read as nothing happening.
- **v20_c03_g3.png · take 3** — Wall lightened correctly, but her hands press flat on bare brick — no curtain anywhere in shot, so the action is unreadable.
- **v20_c03_g5.png · take 5** — Curtain finally present, but hanging as a thin even sheet with her hand beside it rather than gripping it.

### What made it work

Rejected takes — 2 before this one. Take 1 (v20_c03_g1.mp4): sped-up and unnatural. The prompt carried “A CLEARLY DIFFERENT POSE EVERY HALF SECOND” plus extra sub-actions — shakes it out hard twice, stretches up on her toes, walks her hands sideways, right foot then left off the stool. Eight beats crammed into four seconds is what makes a real person look like time-lapse. Take 2 (v20_c03_g2.mp4): pace fixed, but the camera panned right across the clip and she turned at the step-down, showing her face from about 3.0s.A held object needs the grip described, not the object. The take that worked spells out what each hand does — one pinching the header lead, the other squeezing “a dozen strands bundled and compressed together between her fingers” — then says where the rest of the mass goes: down the wall, across her body, pooled on the snow. Wall colour is a lighting decision. White bulbs against near-black brick disappear; the pale greige wall is what makes the product readable at all.

Two simultaneous actions read better than one. Pressing the mesh flat and shaking the bundle out in the same 1.3s window is what fills the beat; a single action over four seconds always comes back static. “Off” needs policing in the video prompt too. “The curtain is COMPLETELY OFF the entire time — not one bulb lights, no glow, no colour, no animation on the wall” is what stops seedance rewarding itself by switching the product on mid-clip.“A different pose every half second” is a crowd instruction, not a person instruction. On a crowd or an animated light sign it produces life; on one person doing physical work it produces time-lapse. For a single human, three unhurried beats across four seconds is the whole clip — and the anti-speed block has to be explicit: “NO fast motion, NO sped-up footage, NO time-lapse, NO jittery or twitchy limbs, NO extra actions squeezed in between the three beats above.” “Seen from behind” does not survive a turn-around beat. Any beat where the subject steps back or looks up invites seedance to swing her round. It takes a named ban list — no profile, no three-quarter front, no cheek, no nose, no eye, no chin, no glance over the shoulder — plus “her chest and face stay pointed at the wall for the entire clip.” Locked framing needs a falsifiable test. “NO PAN LEFT OR RIGHT, NO TILT” on its own is soft; “the wall, the gutter line and the step stool stay in exactly the same place in frame from the first frame to the last — if the wall slides across frame, it is wrong” is what holds it.

REFERENCE IMAGE 2 governs the framing and the phone screen ONLY — do not copy its wall or its background. Framing: night sky across the top 20 percent, the white gutter line at 20 percent down, the base of the wall at 53 percent, snowy lawn from 53 percent to the bottom, a bare tree at the far left, a warm street lamp glowing at the left about 35 percent down, a lit porch corner at the right edge. The screen is the brightest thing in the photo and is crisply legible.

ABSOLUTELY NO: a bare wall with no lights on it, any lit or glowing bulb, any artwork or picture on the wall, an LED video wall or screen on the wall, windows, doors, daylight, text overlay, blur.

### Video prompt

```text
Animate the reference image exactly as it is. The first frame of the video IS the reference image, unchanged — same night, same dark sky, same bare tree at the left, same street lamp glowing, same pale greige brick side wall of the house with the unlit LED curtain hanging flat across it, same lit porch and sconce at the right, same frosted lawn, same hand in the dark sleeve holding a phone up in the lower-right of frame, same app screen titled Christmas Lights showing a grid of small scene tiles with the reindeer tile in the top right ringed in orange.

CAMERA — HELD IPHONE, LOCKED FRAMING. Portrait, handheld micro-shake only. ABSOLUTELY NO ZOOM IN, NO ZOOM OUT, NO PUSH IN, NO PULL BACK, NO DOLLY. The wall and the phone stay exactly the same size in frame throughout.

ACTION TIMELINE (4 seconds, one continuous take, no cuts):
0.0-1.2s — the thumb comes in from the bottom right and scrolls the grid of scene tiles with real inertia: the tiles slide up, a fresh row of new scene thumbnails comes into view from the bottom, the list overshoots slightly and settles back with a small bounce. The phone tilts a few degrees as the hand adjusts its grip.
1.2-1.6s — the thumb reaches out and TAPS one tile. The tapped tile flashes and takes a bright highlight ring.
1.6s — ON THAT EXACT FRAME the entire wall SNAPS ON at full brightness in a single frame. NO fade, NO dissolve, NO ramp, NO brightness curve, NO bulbs lighting one by one, NO sweep across the wall. One frame dark, the next frame fully lit at maximum brightness. The artwork that appears is a Santa in a red suit and hat lounging back in a wooden chair raising a foaming glass of beer, with a row of beer glasses along the bottom, and a large brown reindeer with big antlers standing at the right holding a rein in its mouth and looking distinctly unimpressed. Colours are warm red, cream, gold and brown against the dark unlit wall.
1.6-2.3s — the hand lowers straight down out of the bottom of frame and NEVER returns. The phone leaves frame completely.
2.3-4.0s — the frame holds on the glowing wall and the artwork ANIMATES: at 2.6s Santa tips the glass to his mouth and his cheeks puff; at 3.0s the reindeer's eyes roll and its head turns away; at 3.4s Santa's arm swings the glass back up in a big toast and his mouth opens wide; at 3.8s the reindeer stamps a hoof and the rein whips. Every pose change is rendered by bulbs switching on and off row by row across the grid like an animated light-bulb sign — the figures fully redraw between beats, chunky and instantaneous, never smooth morphing. Coloured light spills and shifts across the frosted lawn below with each change.

CRITICAL — HOW THE LIGHTS MUST BE BUILT. SURFACE OVERRIDE: wherever the block below says "garage door", read "the flat brick side wall of the house". Everything else in the block applies exactly as written.
The picture on the garage door is NOT an LED video wall, NOT a screen, NOT a projection. It is a hanging curtain of LED string lights: many thin vertical wires hung side by side from a header cable pinned under the roof edge, each wire carrying small round bulbs. Build the whole picture out of VERTICAL DOTTED LINES OF LIGHT. The horizontal gap between neighbouring strands is visibly WIDER than the vertical gap between bulbs on the same strand, so the grid is tall and rectangular — never a square pixel matrix.
The curtain is SEE-THROUGH. Through the gaps between strands you clearly read the garage door behind it: its seams, its trim, the faint shadows of the wires falling on it. The unlit areas are NOT a solid coloured field — they are simply dark garage door, with the unlit bulbs showing as tiny dull grey dots and the thin dark wires as faint vertical lines.
Every lit bulb is a tiny point source: blown-out white core, coloured halo bleeding around it, a small soft star flare, individually countable. The artwork is extremely low resolution and blocky — chunky stepped edges, no smooth curves, no thin outlines, no small facial detail, no gradients, no anti-aliasing.
The curtain hangs a few inches proud of the door: the strands sag slightly, each strand ends at a slightly different height so the bottom hem is uneven and wavy, a faint breeze tilts the sheet, and the glow bounces back off the door behind it as a soft halo. Light spills onto the snow in a speckled uneven pattern, not a flat even wash.
ABSOLUTELY NO: LED video wall, TV screen, monitor, jumbotron, projector image, black backing panel, bezel or frame, dense square pixel grid, solid saturated background field, smooth clean vector artwork. Real amateur photo, natural noise, no CGI polish.

HARD RULES: the lit artwork is hard-cropped at all four edges of the curtain — it never grows, shrinks, shifts or warps, and nothing spills onto the roof, the trim, the sky or the porch. NO TEXT, NO LETTERS, NO WORDS, NO NUMBERS, NO SIGNS anywhere in the light artwork, and no captions, subtitles or watermark anywhere in the video. Nobody walks into frame.

AUDIO: natural room sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Quiet cold suburban night ambience, a faint distant car, one soft UI tap, a faint electrical buzz as the wall comes on. No voice.

LOOK: real iPhone UGC at night. Noisy shadows, slight motion blur in the highlights, natural phone screen glow on the fingers, no cinematic grade, no lens flare, no slow motion, no speed ramp.
```

### Rejected takes

- **v20_c04_g2.png · take 2** — Wall came back completely bare. Asking for a “switched-off curtain” reads to the model as no curtain — it renders the absence of light, not the presence of dark hardware.
- **v20_c04_g3.png · take 3** — Curtain present once the strand close-up was attached and the wall was called “ALREADY COVERED, edge to edge”, but the strands sit flat and even against the brick with no sag.

### What made it work

“Switched off” is not a state the model renders — describe the hardware instead. Two takes came back as bare brick. The fix was leading with “THE MOST IMPORTANT THING IN THIS IMAGE: the entire wall is ALREADY COVERED, edge to edge — this is not an empty wall”, then describing the dead bulbs as physical objects: “small dull cold-grey plastic beads, no glow.” Dark hardware needs a light source to exist. “The street lamp rakes across the sheet so the grey beads catch a faint specular highlight” is the line that makes an unlit curtain visible at night.

A snap-on has to ban the alternatives by name. “ON THAT EXACT FRAME the entire wall SNAPS ON at full brightness in a single frame. NO fade, NO dissolve, NO ramp, NO brightness curve, NO bulbs lighting one by one, NO sweep across the wall” gives a true one-frame cut to full. What it does not control is when. The tap was written at 1.2-1.6s and the wall came on nearer 3.0s — seedance treats early timestamps as soft. Next time budget the UI business shorter, or put the tap at 0.8s to buy the lit wall more screen time.

### Rejected takes

- **v20_c05_g3.png · take 3** — Text still there, and the artwork renders as smooth clean vector shapes washed flat over the siding: a projector, not a curtain.

### What made it work

Say what the picture shows, never what it says. Three takes running spelled words across the house because the gag was described in a way that invited a caption. The ban has to be absolute and repeated: “NO TEXT, NO LETTERS, NO WORDS, NO NUMBERS, NO SIGNS anywhere in the light artwork or anywhere in the photo.” Comedy carries better as a situation than as a punchline. Santa slumped in a recliner with a beer and a fed-up reindeer waiting reads instantly from the street; a joke that has to be read does not.

Walking is not zooming, and it has to be said that way. “THE CAMERA ITSELF IS THE PHONE” + “THIS IS WALKING, NOT ZOOMING: no focal length change, no digital zoom, no push-in” + parallax described on the garland stakes is what keeps it from reading as a lens move. The no-phone hard negative is mandatory in a POV. Banning phone, screen, hand, fingers, thumb, arm, sleeve, glove and device from the foreground and the bottom edge kept the frame clean; the earlier “held iPhone” wording put a hand in shot every time.

### Video prompt

```text
Wholesome neighbourhood Christmas scene, warm and jolly. Animate the reference image exactly as it is. The first frame of the video IS the reference image, unchanged — same two-storey house, same brick garage bay, same wet reflective driveway throwing coloured light back, same crowd of neighbours seen FROM BEHIND standing and sitting on the snow in winter coats and bobble hats, same black dog at the left, same two people at the edges holding phones up, same lit LED curtain across the garage door showing Santa sitting in a loaded sleigh raising a foaming glass, a stack of wrapped presents behind him, a blue cooler beside him and a brown reindeer sitting on a bench at the right raising a glass back at him.

THE SINGLE MOST IMPORTANT INSTRUCTION — THE CLIP STARTS MID-ACTION. Everything in this scene is ALREADY MOVING AT FULL SPEED IN THE VERY FIRST FRAME. The crowd is already cheering, already laughing, already waving, and the lights on the garage door are already animating. There is NO still opening frame, NO frozen first second, NO settling-in, NO ease-in, NO slow build, NO moment where the picture holds before anything happens. Frame one is the busiest part of the clip, not the calmest. If any half-second of this video looks like a photograph, it is wrong.

CAMERA — HELD IPHONE, LOCKED FRAMING. Portrait, filmed from behind the group at the bottom of the driveway. Handheld micro-shake only — a few pixels of organic drift. ABSOLUTELY NO ZOOM IN, NO ZOOM OUT, NO PUSH IN, NO PULL BACK, NO DOLLY, NO CRANE. The house and the garage door stay exactly the same size in frame from the first frame to the last.

THE CROWD IS ALIVE FROM FRAME ONE AND NEVER STOPS — a clearly different pose every quarter second, all seen FROM BEHIND, no face ever turned toward camera. The biggest actions come FIRST:
0.0-0.25s — ALREADY IN PROGRESS: the child in the red coat is mid-jump with both arms thrown straight up, the woman in the centre is already doubled over laughing with her shoulders shaking, three people in the back row already have their arms overhead waving, the black dog is already up on all fours barking at the garage.
0.25-0.5s — the child lands and immediately bounces again; the man at the right punches his phone hand higher and steps in; two heads snap round to each other.
0.5-0.75s — the whole seated front row rocks forward together and claps hard; the dog shakes itself head to tail.
0.75-1.0s — two people high-five over the heads in front; a woman at the left throws her head back, breath fogging.
1.0-1.5s — a child in a cream bobble hat twists round to tug a sleeve and swings back; someone lifts a takeaway cup and jabs it at the garage door.
1.5-2.0s — the back row cheers again with arms going up raggedly, not in unison; a kid scrambles up onto their knees; the dog barks twice more.
2.0-2.5s — the man at the right lowers his phone, wipes his eyes laughing and claps; a woman leans back hard into the person beside her.
2.5-3.0s — the front row all point at once, arms out at different heights; two people bounce on the spot.
3.0-3.5s — sustained ragged applause spreading across the whole group, hats bobbing, breath fogging, someone waves an arm overhead in a big slow arc.
3.5-4.0s — still applauding, the child in the red coat jumps one more time, the dog sits back down and the man at the right raises his phone again.
Coats crease, hats bob, breath fogs, snow compresses under boots. NOBODY IS EVER STILL — at every single moment of the four seconds at least six different people are visibly moving, and no two of them are doing the same thing.

THE ARTWORK ANIMATES FROM FRAME ONE TOO, redrawing fully between beats, and its FIRST change happens at 0.2s — not later: at 0.2s Santa tips his glass to his mouth and his cheeks puff; at 0.6s the reindeer clinks its glass forward and its ears flick; at 1.0s Santa raises both arms in a big toast and the stack of presents wobbles; at 1.5s the reindeer's eyes roll and it slumps back on the bench; at 2.0s Santa kicks a boot up over the front of the sleigh; at 2.5s the cooler lid flaps open and shut; at 3.0s the reindeer stamps and its antlers tilt; at 3.5s both of them clink glasses together and Santa's hat flies up. Every pose change is rendered by bulbs switching on and off row by row across the grid like an animated light-bulb sign — chunky and instantaneous, never smooth morphing. The coloured reflections on the wet driveway shift and ripple with every change.

CRITICAL — HOW THE LIGHTS MUST BE BUILT.
The picture on the garage door is NOT an LED video wall, NOT a screen, NOT a projection. It is a hanging curtain of LED string lights: many thin vertical wires hung side by side from a header cable pinned under the garage lintel, each wire carrying small round bulbs. Build the whole picture out of VERTICAL DOTTED LINES OF LIGHT. The horizontal gap between neighbouring strands is visibly WIDER than the vertical gap between bulbs on the same strand, so the grid is tall and rectangular — never a square pixel matrix.
The curtain is SEE-THROUGH. Through the gaps between strands you clearly read the garage door behind it: its horizontal panel seams, its rim of trim, the faint shadows of the wires falling on it. The unlit areas are NOT a solid coloured field — they are simply dark garage door, with the unlit bulbs showing as tiny dull grey dots and the thin dark wires as faint vertical lines.
Every lit bulb is a tiny point source: blown-out white core, coloured halo bleeding around it, a small soft star flare, individually countable. The artwork is extremely low resolution and blocky — chunky stepped edges, no smooth curves, no thin outlines, no small facial detail, no gradients, no anti-aliasing.
The curtain hangs a few inches proud of the door: the strands sag slightly, each strand ends at a slightly different height so the bottom hem is uneven and wavy, a faint breeze tilts the sheet, and the glow bounces back off the door behind it as a soft halo. Light spills onto the snow in a speckled uneven pattern, not a flat even wash.
ABSOLUTELY NO: LED video wall, TV screen, monitor, jumbotron, projector image, black backing panel, bezel or frame, dense square pixel grid, solid saturated background field, smooth clean vector artwork. Real amateur photo, natural noise, no CGI polish.

ABSOLUTE HARD RULE — THE GARAGE DOOR IS THE ONLY LIT SURFACE. The artwork is hard-cropped at all four edges of the curtain. It NEVER grows, shrinks, shifts, warps or drifts. Nothing spills onto the brick, the gables, the roof, the sky or the neighbouring house. No limb of Santa or the reindeer ever crosses outside the curtain rectangle.

HARD RULES: nobody turns around, no face is shown toward camera. No new people walk into frame. NO TEXT, NO LETTERS, NO WORDS, NO NUMBERS, NO SIGNS anywhere in the light artwork, and no captions, subtitles or watermark anywhere in the video. No speed ramp, no slow motion, no freeze frame, no still opening.

AUDIO: natural room sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Cold outdoor night ambience, a happy crowd already laughing and cheering as the clip opens, children shouting, dog barks, scattered applause, boots on wet snow, a faint electrical hum from the lights.

LOOK: real iPhone UGC at night. Noisy shadows, blown highlights around the bulbs, natural handheld wobble, no cinematic grade, no lens flare, no slow motion, no speed ramp.
```

### Rejected takes

- **v20_c06_g1.png · take 1** — Lettering again, and the picture is a soft glow painted onto the garage door with no visible bulbs at all.
- **v20_c06_g3.png · take 3** — Text still present; artwork is smooth vector line-work with anti-aliased curves and thin outlines — no bulb grid, no door readable behind it.

### What made it work

Rejected take — 1 before this one. (v20_c06_g1.mp4): almost no movement for the first two seconds, everything back-loaded into the last second. Frame-difference over the clip: 0.67 in the opening second against 1.60 in the closing second — seedance eased in from the still start frame and only woke up at the end.The anti-panel block is the only thing that stops it becoming a screen. Without it the curtain renders as smooth vector art washed over the door. Pasted verbatim it produces the real thing: vertical dotted lines with a wider horizontal gap than vertical, the garage door seams readable through the gaps, blocky stepped edges, no anti-aliasing. Low resolution has to be requested as a feature. “Extremely low resolution and blocky — no smooth curves, no thin outlines, no gradients” is what makes it read as bulbs rather than pixels.

A crowd needs a named person per beat. Eight half-second beats each naming who moves — the child in the red coat, the woman in the centre, the black dog, the man at the right with the phone — is what produces a live crowd; “the crowd cheers” produces statues. Stagger the applause. “arms going up raggedly, not in unison” is the line that stops the whole front row moving as one puppet.Half-second beats do not stop seedance easing in. The model treats a still start frame as a resting state and builds toward motion, so a beat list that starts at 0.0s still comes back dead for two seconds. The fix is to declare the clip already in progress. “THE CLIP STARTS MID-ACTION — everything is ALREADY MOVING AT FULL SPEED IN THE VERY FIRST FRAME… frame one is the busiest part of the clip, not the calmest. If any half-second of this video looks like a photograph, it is wrong”, then quarter-second beats through the opening second with the biggest actions first and every one phrased as already happening — mid-jump, already doubled over laughing, already up on all fours barking. That flattened the motion curve from 0.67 / 1.60 (first second vs last) to 1.19 / 1.18.

### Rejected takes

- **v20_c07_g1.png · take 1** — Lettering, and the gag was too crude — past “cheeky” and into something that cannot run as an ad.
- **v20_c07_g3.png · take 3** — Text still present, and the artwork sits as a flat smooth panel on the gable with a solid saturated background field instead of dark siding.

### What made it work

When the surface is not a garage door, override the block rather than rewrite it. One line does it: “SURFACE OVERRIDE: wherever the block below says ‘garage door’, read ‘gable end siding wall’… everything else applies exactly as written.” Editing the block itself is how it drifts. Controversial has a ceiling. Santa raiding the fridge with milk straight from the carton lands; the take-1 gag was past what can run as an ad. Caught-in-the-act beats crude.

A drive-by needs a direction stated as a size change. “THE CAR IS DRIVING FORWARD and closing in on the house, so the gable wall grows steadily and continuously LARGER across the four seconds and NEVER recedes” — the “never recedes” clause is what stops seedance reversing the move halfway. Let the car be the only foreground. Keeping the window frame and wing mirror as named permitted objects, with everything else banned, gives real vehicle parallax without a hand creeping in.

CAMERA — HELD IPHONE, LOCKED FOCAL LENGTH. A low three-quarter angle from the driveway, portrait. Handheld micro-shake only — a few pixels of organic drift. ABSOLUTELY NO ZOOM IN, NO ZOOM OUT, NO PUSH IN, NO PULL BACK, NO DOLLY, NO CRANE, NO ORBIT. The wall, the roof edge and the stool stay exactly the same size in frame from the first frame to the last.

MOVEMENT — A CLEARLY DIFFERENT POSE EVERY HALF SECOND. She is working hard the whole four seconds; no beat is static. Big whole-body actions: reaching, stretching, pressing, shaking, stepping. Her hair swings, her jacket creases, her weight shifts on the stool.

ACTION TIMELINE (4 seconds, one continuous take, no cuts):
0.0-1.3s — she presses the gathered mesh flat against the brick with her left palm and runs it upward toward the gutter, then lifts the bundle in her right hand and SHAKES it out hard twice — the tangled net drops open and a long sheet of strands falls loose down the wall, the loose coil on the snow uncoiling and shifting with a visible lurch.
1.3-2.6s — she stretches up on her toes, both arms high, and pushes the header wire up under the lip of the gutter, thumbs working along it; then she walks her hands sideways to the right along the roofline, pressing and tucking as she goes, her shoulders rolling, her torso twisting, heels lifting and dropping on the stool tread. 2.6-4.0s — she steps down off the stool, right foot then left, onto the snow, takes one step back away from the wall, plants her hands on her hips, and tilts her head right back to look up at the full height of what she has just hung, chin lifting, hair falling back off her shoulders. The whole curtain sways gently against the brick and settles.

HARD RULES: the curtain is COMPLETELY OFF the entire time — not one bulb lights, no glow, no colour, no animation on the wall. The wall never gains windows, doors or new features. The strands are separate vertical strands obeying gravity, sagging slightly, with an uneven wavy bottom hem — never a flat sheet, never a panel, never a screen. The loose coil rests in the snow with real contact shadows. Her face is never shown; she stays turned away from camera throughout. No other people enter frame. NO TEXT, NO LETTERS, NO WORDS, NO NUMBERS, NO CAPTIONS, NO SUBTITLES, NO WATERMARK anywhere in the video.

AUDIO: natural room sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Cold outdoor winter ambience, thin plastic strands rattling and clicking against brick, a step-stool creak, sneakers on a metal tread, boots compressing snow, one quiet effortful breath. No voice.

LOOK: real iPhone UGC. Flat overcast winter daylight, slightly cool white balance, mild sensor noise, natural skin, no cinematic grade, no lens flare, no slow motion, no speed ramp.

**Shot notes**

- `c01` - Regenerate by naming the single change, not by rewriting the prompt. The take-3 prompt is just take 1 fed back as @Image 1 plus “CHANGE ONE THING ONLY: RAISE THE CAMERA…” and an explicit description of the new space — “the TOP QUARTER OF THE FRAME IS CLEAN EMPTY OVERCAST SKY with nothing in it.” Naming the headroom as an object is what produced it; asking for “more headroom” does not.
- `c02` - Three references, three jobs, each spelled out before any description starts. Composition from @Image 1, face from @Image 2, box art from @Image 3 — plus “ignore its room, its colours, its text overlay and its box artwork” so the framing reference cannot leak content. Empty space has to be described as a thing. “COMPLETELY EMPTY AND CLEAR — a long unbroken stretch of bare cushion with nothing resting on it: no boxes, no bags, no blankets” is what finally cleared the seat; “an empty sofa” never did.
- `c03` - Rejected takes — 2 before this one. Take 1 (v20_c03_g1.mp4): sped-up and unnatural. The prompt carried “A CLEARLY DIFFERENT POSE EVERY HALF SECOND” plus extra sub-actions — shakes it out hard twice, stretches up on her toes, walks her hands sideways, right foot then left off the stool. Eight beats crammed into four seconds is what makes a real person look like time-lapse. Take 2 (v20_c03_g2.mp4): pace fixed, but the camera panned right across the clip and she turned at the step-down, showing her face from about 3.0s.
- `c04` - “Switched off” is not a state the model renders — describe the hardware instead. Two takes came back as bare brick. The fix was leading with “THE MOST IMPORTANT THING IN THIS IMAGE: the entire wall is ALREADY COVERED, edge to edge — this is not an empty wall”, then describing the dead bulbs as physical objects: “small dull cold-grey plastic beads, no glow.” Dark hardware needs a light source to exist. “The street lamp rakes across the sheet so the grey beads catch a faint specular highlight” is the line that makes an unlit curtain visible at night.
- `c05` - Say what the picture shows, never what it says. Three takes running spelled words across the house because the gag was described in a way that invited a caption. The ban has to be absolute and repeated: “NO TEXT, NO LETTERS, NO WORDS, NO NUMBERS, NO SIGNS anywhere in the light artwork or anywhere in the photo.” Comedy carries better as a situation than as a punchline. Santa slumped in a recliner with a beer and a fed-up reindeer waiting reads instantly from the street; a joke that has to be read does not.
- `c06` - Rejected take — 1 before this one. (v20_c06_g1.mp4): almost no movement for the first two seconds, everything back-loaded into the last second. Frame-difference over the clip: 0.67 in the opening second against 1.60 in the closing second — seedance eased in from the still start frame and only woke up at the end.
- `c07` - When the surface is not a garage door, override the block rather than rewrite it. One line does it: “SURFACE OVERRIDE: wherever the block below says ‘garage door’, read ‘gable end siding wall’… everything else applies exactly as written.” Editing the block itself is how it drifts. Controversial has a ceiling. Santa raiding the fridge with milk straight from the carton lands; the take-1 gag was past what can run as an ad. Caught-in-the-act beats crude.

CHANGE ONE THING ONLY: RAISE THE CAMERA. The camera is lifted roughly 40-50 cm higher than in the reference and tilted very slightly downward, so the whole scene sits LOWER in the vertical frame. The result must leave a clean, clearly EMPTY BAND across the TOP 20-25% of the frame - plain overcast sky and the upper roofline only, with no head, no important detail and no clutter in it - because a text overlay will be placed there. The woman must still be fully inside the frame from the top of her head down to her boots, and the top of her head must sit noticeably below the top edge with obvious empty space above it. Do not change her pose, her clothing, the Santa, the pump, the house or the lighting.

Vertical 9:16 phone-video still. Extremely high quality, hyper realistic, crisp real-estate-photography clarity, evenly exposed, sharp focus front to back, fine natural texture in the snow, the jacket fabric and the vinyl of the inflatable. NOT cinematic, no colour grading, no vignette, no shallow depth of field, no bokeh, no haze, no bloom, no motion blur, no CGI polish. Real amateur phone photo.
```

Match the CROP AND COMPOSITION of REFERENCE IMAGE 1 exactly. REFERENCE IMAGE 1 (the phone screenshot) governs FRAMING ONLY - ignore its room, its colours, its text overlay and its box artwork. REFERENCE IMAGE 2 governs the WOMAN's face and hair. REFERENCE IMAGE 3 governs the BOX artwork.

THE CROP IS THE MOST IMPORTANT THING. Vertical 9:16 handheld phone-video still, indoors at night. The camera is CLOSE to her. Her body is CUT OFF BY THE LEFT EDGE of the frame - her left shoulder and left side are outside the picture entirely. What is inside the frame is her head, her right shoulder, her chest and both arms, filling the whole left half of the picture from top to bottom. The crown of her head is right up near the top edge of the frame with only a small gap of headroom, and the bottom of the frame cuts her at about mid-thigh. She is BIG in frame - do NOT show her whole body, do NOT stand the camera back, do NOT show her standing small in a wide room.

She is standing in near profile, turned to her right, head tilted down looking at the box in her hands, so the camera sees the back-left three-quarter of her head - long dark brown hair falling forward over her shoulder and down her back, only a sliver of cheek, NO clear full face. She wears a soft oversized cream ivory crew-neck sweatshirt and matching cream sweatpants.

BOTH HANDS ARE ON THE BOX AND THE BOX IS IN MID-AIR. Far hand at the far top corner of the box, near hand gripping the near lower edge, both forearms reaching out and down in front of her at waist height. The box hangs over the sofa with a clear gap of several inches beneath it - it is NOT resting on anything. It lies tilted diagonally across the CENTRE of the frame, printed front face angled up toward the camera, large and legible, spanning most of the frame width because it is about 55 cm wide, wider than her shoulders.

SCENE. Lower right third: the arm and seat cushions of a large oatmeal-cream fabric sectional sofa with a chunky cream knit throw and a red-and-cream plaid cushion. Behind her a warmly decorated living room at night - a stone fireplace with garland and warm fairy lights at the left behind her shoulder, a tall Christmas tree with warm white lights and red and gold ornaments at the right, warm-white walls, pale wide-plank oak floor, a brass floor lamp. Warm cosy light, many small fairy lights.

THE BOX, exactly as in REFERENCE IMAGE 3: matte black rigid retail box, large and flat, about 55 cm wide by 42 cm tall by 10 cm deep. Front panel top to bottom - large white condensed uppercase headline LED CURTAIN LIGHTS; warm gold subline Create Your Own Magic; red diagonal BEST SELLER ribbon flag across the top-right corner; one large photographic panel with a thin gold hairline border filling the middle and lower two thirds showing a night photo of a suburban house facade with a glowing pixel LED Christmas tree on the garage door and warm fairy-lit trees either side; bottom row of five small gold line icons with white captions reading DIY Designs, App Control, IP65 Waterproof, Timer Function, Connectable. THE ARTWORK ON THE CURTAIN: a curtain of LED string lights hangs across the whole gable end of the house, from just under the roof peak down past the upper windows. It displays a crude, blocky, very low-resolution cartoon: a fat red Santa stuck head-first down a brick chimney with only his legs and oversized black boots kicking in the air above the chimney pot, one boot flung off and flying, square presents spilling down the roof, and beneath it in chunky capital letters made of lit bulbs the words SEND HELP. Red, white, green and gold bulbs only, everything built from separated points of light.

SURFACE OVERRIDE: wherever the block below says "garage door", read "the house's gable siding behind the curtain" — the curtain hangs on the gable wall, not on a garage door. Everything else in the block applies exactly as written.

CRITICAL — HOW THE LIGHTS MUST BE BUILT. The picture on the garage door is NOT an LED video wall, NOT a screen, NOT a projection. The unlit areas are NOT a solid coloured field — they are simply dark garage door, with the unlit bulbs showing as tiny dull grey dots and the thin dark wires as faint vertical lines.

Every lit bulb is a tiny point source: blown-out white core, coloured halo bleeding around it, a small soft star flare, individually countable. ABSOLUTELY NO: LED video wall, TV screen, monitor, jumbotron, projector image, black backing panel, bezel or frame, dense square pixel grid, solid saturated background field, smooth clean vector artwork. Real amateur photo, natural noise, no CGI polish.

Big chunky comic shapes, read instantly from the street.

NO TEXT, NO LETTERS, NO WORDS, NO NUMBERS, NO SIGNS anywhere in the light artwork or anywhere in the photo.

The horizontal gap between neighbouring strands is visibly WIDER than the vertical gap between bulbs on the same strand, so the grid is tall and rectangular — never a square pixel matrix.

The curtain is SEE-THROUGH. Through the gaps between strands you clearly read the white garage door behind it: its horizontal panel seams, its rim of trim, the faint shadows of the wires falling on it. The unlit areas are NOT a solid coloured field — they are simply dark garage door, with the unlit bulbs showing as tiny dull grey dots and the thin dark wires as faint vertical lines.

Every lit bulb is a tiny point source: blown-out white core, coloured halo bleeding around it, a small soft star flare, individually countable. The artwork is extremely low resolution and blocky — chunky stepped edges, no smooth curves, no thin outlines, no small facial detail, no gradients, no anti-aliasing.

The curtain hangs a few inches proud of the door: the strands sag slightly, each strand ends at a slightly different height so the bottom hem is uneven and wavy, a faint breeze tilts the sheet, and the glow bounces back off the door behind it as a soft halo. Light spills onto the snow in a speckled uneven pattern, not a flat even wash.

ABSOLUTELY NO: LED video wall, TV screen, monitor, jumbotron, projector image, black backing panel, bezel or frame, dense square pixel grid, solid saturated background field, smooth clean vector artwork. Real amateur photo, natural noise, no CGI polish.

Animate the reference image exactly as it is. The first frame of the video IS the reference image, unchanged — same snowy driveway, same open garage full of clutter, same white house with the 3217 number plate and coach lamp, same slumped red-and-green inflatable Santa lying collapsed face-up on the wet plowed driveway, same tall black hand pump with its black hose running to the Santa, same woman in the black puffer jacket, cream knitted headband, black leggings and tan fur-topped snow boots.

CAMERA — HELD IPHONE, LOCKED FOCAL LENGTH. A friend is standing on the driveway filming her on a phone in portrait. The framing never changes size: NO ZOOM IN, NO ZOOM OUT, NO PUSH IN, NO PULL BACK, NO DOLLY, NO CRANE, NO ORBIT. Handheld micro-shake only — a few pixels of organic drift, the kind a real hand makes. The house, the garage opening and the Santa stay exactly the same size in frame from the first frame to the last.

MOVEMENT — A CLEARLY DIFFERENT POSE EVERY HALF SECOND. Her body never rests for a single frame. Her FACE fully redraws between beats — eyebrows, eyes and mouth are completely different shapes from one beat to the next: effort, hope, doubt, disgust, fury. Big whole-body actions, not small gestures.

ACTION TIMELINE (5 seconds, one continuous take, no cuts):
0.0-0.6s — she drives the pump handle DOWN hard with both hands, shoulders dropping, elbows locking out, knees bending, breath fogging out of her mouth, jaw clenched.
0.6-1.2s — the handle rips back UP and her whole torso lifts with it, hair swinging, then slams DOWN again even harder. Two full fast strokes inside this window.
1.2-2.0s — third and fourth strokes, faster and more violent, her boots shifting for grip on the icy driveway, her weight thrown into each push. The Santa reacts: the red belly rounds out a little and the left arm lifts a few inches off the ground, the vinyl rippling and trembling. It inflates only a LITTLE. It never stands up, it never becomes full, it stays collapsed on the driveway for the entire video.
2.0-2.6s — she stops dead, straightens up, stares down at the pathetic half-lumpy Santa with her mouth open and her eyebrows slammed together, chest heaving. The Santa's arm sags straight back down and the belly deflates again with a visible wobble.
2.6-3.4s — she SHOVES the pump away from her with both hands and it topples over onto the snow; she throws both arms out sideways in a furious "are you kidding me" gesture and shouts, mouth wide open, head thrown forward.
3.4-4.2s — she takes one big step forward and KICKS the inflatable hard with her right boot — a full committed swing from the hip, arms flying up for balance, her whole body twisting through the kick. The vinyl caves in at the impact point and the whole Santa slides and flops sideways across the wet driveway, the hose whipping after it.
4.2-5.0s — she plants both feet, rakes the hair off her face, jabs a finger down at the Santa and screams again, red-faced, then throws both hands up and turns half away in disgust.

HARD RULES: the Santa NEVER fully inflates and NEVER stands upright — it only puffs slightly, then collapses again. The pump hose stays attached to the Santa the whole time. No other people enter the frame. The house, the garage contents and the parked items never change or move. NO TEXT, NO LETTERS, NO WORDS, NO NUMBERS, NO CAPTIONS, NO SUBTITLES, NO WATERMARK anywhere in the video.

AUDIO: natural room sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Cold outdoor winter ambience, the wheeze and thud of a hand pump, vinyl crinkling, boots scraping on wet ice, one furious wordless female shout, a dull thump on the kick.

LOOK: real iPhone UGC. Flat overcast winter daylight, slightly cool white balance, mild sensor noise in the shadows, natural skin, no cinematic grade, no lens flare, no slow motion, no speed ramp.
````

REFERENCE IMAGES. Reference image 1 is the first frame of scene 1 — start there, unchanged. Reference image 2 is the CLOSED PRODUCT BOX: the artwork on the box in this video must match it exactly. Reference image 3 is the OPEN BOX INTERIOR: what she reveals in scene 2 must match it exactly.

BOX SPEC — TRUE IN EVERY FRAME. A matte black rigid retail box, 55 cm wide x 42 cm tall x 10 cm deep. It is BIG — much wider than the span of two outstretched hands; each of her hands covers less than a quarter of the box width, and laid on the sofa seat it spans most of one cushion. Front panel top to bottom: a large white condensed uppercase headline LED CURTAIN LIGHTS; a warm gold subline Create Your Own Magic; a red diagonal BEST SELLER ribbon flag in the top-right corner; one large photographic panel with a thin gold hairline border showing a night house facade with a glowing pixel LED Christmas tree on the garage door; a bottom row of five small gold line icons with white captions. NO transparent window, NO clear plastic panel, NO die-cut cutout, NO product visible through the front, NO glowing lights showing through the cardboard. The box is closed and sealed until the moment she opens it in scene 2.

SCENE 1 — 0.0 to 2.5s. CAMERA: HELD IPHONE, LOCKED FOCAL LENGTH, portrait, a friend filming from where the reference image was taken. NO ZOOM IN, NO ZOOM OUT, NO PUSH IN, NO DOLLY. Handheld micro-shake only. The living room behind her — stone fireplace with hanging stockings, the two lit Christmas trees, the lamp, the cream sofa, the wood floor — stays exactly as it is and never changes.
THE WHOLE POINT OF SCENE 1: she carries the box round in front of the sofa, sets it flat on the seat cushion AND drops to her knees IN THE SAME CONTINUOUS MOTION. These are not two separate steps — the box going down and her body going down are one move.
0.0-0.5s — she takes a step forward and to her right, turning her shoulders, swinging the heavy box out in front of her; her hair swings across, her cream fleece sleeve creases with the weight.
0.5-1.3s — she steps in front of the sofa, leans forward from the hips and lowers the box toward the empty seat cushion, arms extending, knees already bending underneath her.
1.3-1.9s — the box lands flat and printed-side-up on the cushion with a soft compression of the cushion, her hands still on it, and in the same motion her knees come down onto the wood floor, one after the other, her weight settling, her back rounding forward.
1.9-2.5s — kneeling now, she shuffles closer to the sofa, slides the box squarely in front of her with both hands, pushes her hair behind one ear and plants both palms flat on the box lid, chest rising with an excited breath.

HARD CUT AT 2.5s.

SCENE 2 — 2.5 to 5.0s. Direct continuation, same room, same second, same woman, same clothes, same box in exactly the same place on the same cushion. The camera has moved about 30 degrees round to her side — a slightly more side-on three-quarter angle, a little lower, closer to the level of the sofa seat, so we now see the lid edge in profile and read the pivot clearly. Still HELD IPHONE, LOCKED FOCAL LENGTH, handheld micro-shake only, NO ZOOM.
LID MECHANISM, GEOMETRICALLY. The box is a magnetic-closure flip-top. The hinge runs along the TOP LONG EDGE of the box — the far edge away from her, at the back. The lid pivots UP and BACK on that hinge, rotating away from her and toward the back of the sofa, keeping its printed front face turned toward the camera as it rises. The inside of the lid is matte black. The lid is NEVER lifted off, NEVER removed, NEVER carried aside, NEVER set down, NEVER folded flat instantly, NEVER slid out like a drawer.
2.5-3.0s — she hooks her fingertips under the near front edge of the lid; a tiny snap as the magnets release and the front edge lifts a centimetre, a thin black shadow line opening across the whole 55 cm width.
3.0-4.0s — she pivots the big printed lid UP and BACK on its hinge in one smooth continuous arc with real cardboard weight, her elbows rising, her shoulders lifting, until it stands up roughly vertical. It travels slowly and heavily — it does not flip open instantly.
4.0-4.6s — the interior is revealed exactly as reference image 3: the neatly folded black LED mesh curtain in its tray with the coiled lead and the small accessory items. She reaches in with both hands and touches the folded mesh, fingers spreading it slightly.
4.6-5.0s — she lifts her chin, looks off toward the fireplace wall and breaks into a big open-mouthed grin, eyebrows up, then looks back down into the box.

MOVEMENT RULE FOR BOTH SCENES: a clearly different pose every half second. Her face fully redraws between beats — eyes, eyebrows and mouth completely different shapes from one beat to the next. Hair, sleeves and the sofa cushion all react to her weight.

HARD RULES: exactly ONE cut, at 2.5s. The box never changes size, never changes artwork, never gains a window or a cutout. Nobody else enters frame. NO TEXT, NO LETTERS, NO WORDS, NO NUMBERS, NO CAPTIONS, NO SUBTITLES, NO WATERMARK anywhere in the video.

AUDIO: natural room sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Quiet warm living-room ambience, the soft thump of a heavy box on a cushion, knees on a wood floor, the click of magnets releasing, stiff cardboard flexing, plastic mesh rustling, one small delighted exhale.

LOOK: real iPhone UGC indoors at night. Warm mixed lamp and fairy light, slight sensor noise in the shadows, natural skin, no cinematic grade, no slow motion, no speed ramp.

REFERENCE IMAGE 1 is the shot to reproduce - keep its framing, its camera angle, its woman, her face, her hair, her clothing, her pose, the black folding step stool, the light warm greige painted brick windowless wall, the white roof trim, the snow, the distant neighbouring house and bare trees at the right edge, and the flat overcast winter daylight. Lit garland-wrapped shrubs and small trees along both path edges in the bottom third. Night sky top 15%, snow and path bottom 35%. Change the house style, the neighbours and the background — different architecture, brick instead of siding, different trees — but keep camera height, lens, lantern position and layout identical to the reference.

WHAT THE LIGHTS SHOW — a funny cheeky Christmas scene built ONLY from lit bulbs, with NO writing at all. Santa slouched deep in a recliner with his boots off and socked feet on the footrest, tipping a giant frothy beer mug up to his mouth, foam running down his beard, hat slipping sideways off his head. A stack of empty mugs beside the chair. One reindeer leaning in from the side with the reins in its mouth, eyebrow raised, clearly fed up with waiting. Bold chunky comic shapes, instantly readable from the street.

NO TEXT, NO LETTERS, NO WORDS, NO NUMBERS, NO SIGNS anywhere in the light artwork or anywhere in the photo.

REFERENCE IMAGE 1 governs ONLY the physical construction of the light curtain — wires, bulbs, spacing, see-through quality. Take nothing else from it. REFERENCE IMAGE 2 governs ONLY camera framing and layout.

SURFACE OVERRIDE: wherever the block below says "garage door", read "flat front facade wall of the house" — the curtain hangs on the flat front wall of the house, not on a garage door. Everything else in the block applies exactly as written.

It is a hanging curtain of LED string lights, physically identical to REFERENCE IMAGE 1: many thin vertical wires hung side by side from a header cable pinned under the garage lintel, each wire carrying small round bulbs. Build the whole picture out of VERTICAL DOTTED LINES OF LIGHT. The horizontal gap between neighbouring strands is visibly WIDER than the vertical gap between bulbs on the same strand, so the grid is tall and rectangular — never a square pixel matrix.

The curtain is SEE-THROUGH. Through the gaps between strands you clearly read the white garage door behind it: its horizontal panel seams, its rim of trim, the faint shadows of the wires falling on it. The unlit areas are NOT a solid coloured field — they are simply dark garage door, with the unlit bulbs showing as tiny dull grey dots and the thin dark wires as faint vertical lines.

Every lit bulb is a tiny point source: blown-out white core, coloured halo bleeding around it, a small soft star flare, individually countable. The artwork is extremely low resolution and blocky — chunky stepped edges, no smooth curves, no thin outlines, no small facial detail, no gradients, no anti-aliasing.

The curtain hangs a few inches proud of the door: the strands sag slightly, each strand ends at a slightly different height so the bottom hem is uneven and wavy, a faint breeze tilts the sheet, and the glow bounces back off the door behind it as a soft halo. Light spills onto the snow in a speckled uneven pattern, not a flat even wash.

ABSOLUTELY NO: LED video wall, TV screen, monitor, jumbotron, projector image, black backing panel, bezel or frame, dense square pixel grid, solid saturated background field, smooth clean vector artwork. Real amateur photo, natural noise, no CGI polish.

0.0-0.5s — the child in the red coat who is already pointing jabs their arm higher and bounces on the spot; two heads turn to each other.
0.5-1.0s — a woman in the centre throws her head back laughing, shoulders shaking; the man at the right raises his phone higher and steps in.
1.0-1.5s — the seated kids in the front row all rock forward together and one claps; the black dog stands up, shakes itself and its tail swings.
1.5-2.0s — a woman at the left leans back into the person beside her, breath fogging; another lifts a takeaway cup and points with it.
2.0-2.5s — two people high-five; a child in a cream bobble hat twists round to tug a sleeve and then swings back.
2.5-3.0s — the whole front row cheers, arms going up raggedly, not in unison; the dog barks once toward the garage.
3.0-3.5s — the man at the right lowers his phone, wipes his eyes laughing, and claps; a kid scrambles up onto their knees.
3.5-4.0s — several people applaud, hats bobbing, breath fogging, someone waves an arm overhead, the dog sits back down.
Coats crease, hats bob, breath fogs, snow compresses under boots. Nobody stands still.

THE ARTWORK ANIMATES TOO, in matching beats, redrawing fully between them: at 0.6s Santa tips his glass to his mouth and his cheeks puff; at 1.4s the reindeer clinks its glass forward and its ears flick; at 2.2s Santa raises both arms in a big toast and the stack of presents wobbles; at 3.0s the reindeer's eyes roll and it slumps back on the bench; at 3.6s both of them clink glasses together and the cooler lid flaps. Every pose change is rendered by bulbs switching on and off row by row across the grid like an animated light-bulb sign — chunky and instantaneous, never smooth morphing. The coloured reflections on the wet driveway shift and ripple with every change.

CRITICAL — HOW THE LIGHTS MUST BE BUILT.
The picture on the garage door is NOT an LED video wall, NOT a screen, NOT a projection. It is a hanging curtain of LED string lights: many thin vertical wires hung side by side from a header cable pinned under the garage lintel, each wire carrying small round bulbs. Build the whole picture out of VERTICAL DOTTED LINES OF LIGHT. The horizontal gap between neighbouring strands is visibly WIDER than the vertical gap between bulbs on the same strand, so the grid is tall and rectangular — never a square pixel matrix.
The curtain is SEE-THROUGH. Through the gaps between strands you clearly read the garage door behind it: its horizontal panel seams, its rim of trim, the faint shadows of the wires falling on it. The unlit areas are NOT a solid coloured field — they are simply dark garage door, with the unlit bulbs showing as tiny dull grey dots and the thin dark wires as faint vertical lines.
Every lit bulb is a tiny point source: blown-out white core, coloured halo bleeding around it, a small soft star flare, individually countable. The artwork is extremely low resolution and blocky — chunky stepped edges, no smooth curves, no thin outlines, no small facial detail, no gradients, no anti-aliasing.
The curtain hangs a few inches proud of the door: the strands sag slightly, each strand ends at a slightly different height so the bottom hem is uneven and wavy, a faint breeze tilts the sheet, and the glow bounces back off the door behind it as a soft halo. Light spills onto the snow in a speckled uneven pattern, not a flat even wash.
ABSOLUTELY NO: LED video wall, TV screen, monitor, jumbotron, projector image, black backing panel, bezel or frame, dense square pixel grid, solid saturated background field, smooth clean vector artwork. Real amateur photo, natural noise, no CGI polish.

ABSOLUTE HARD RULE — THE GARAGE DOOR IS THE ONLY LIT SURFACE. The artwork is hard-cropped at all four edges of the curtain. It NEVER grows, shrinks, shifts, warps or drifts. Nothing spills onto the brick, the gables, the roof, the sky or the neighbouring house. No limb of Santa or the reindeer ever crosses outside the curtain rectangle.

HARD RULES: nobody turns around, no face is shown toward camera. No new people walk into frame. NO TEXT, NO LETTERS, NO WORDS, NO NUMBERS, NO SIGNS anywhere in the light artwork, and no captions, subtitles or watermark anywhere in the video.

AUDIO: natural room sound only — no music, no score, no background song. Cold outdoor night ambience, a happy crowd laughing and cheering, children shouting, one dog bark, scattered applause, boots on wet snow, a faint electrical hum from the lights.

LOOK: real iPhone UGC at night. Noisy shadows, blown highlights around the bulbs, natural handheld wobble, no cinematic grade, no lens flare, no slow motion, no speed ramp.

````text
Hyper-realistic amateur iPhone night photo shot from inside a car through the open passenger window, handheld, extremely sharp, no blur, natural high-ISO grain.

FRAMING — copy REFERENCE IMAGE 2 exactly. Dark car interior framing the LEFT EDGE (A-pillar and door trim) and the BOTTOM-LEFT corner (window sill and door card) in near silhouette. Wet dark road across the bottom third with long smeared reflections of the coloured lights. Warm-lit houses receding along the right. Night sky top 20%. Change the house, street and background from the reference — different architecture, different colour, different trees — but keep the car-interior framing, camera position and layout identical.

WHAT THE LIGHTS SHOW — a funny cheeky Christmas scene built ONLY from lit bulbs, with NO writing at all. Santa sneaking out of a lit fridge with an armful of loot: a beer can in one hand, a whole turkey leg in the other, a cookie clamped in his teeth, tiptoeing away on exaggerated pointed toes, fridge door still swinging open behind him spilling light across the floor. An elf peeks around the doorframe behind him, arms crossed, having caught him in the act. Big chunky comic shapes, readable instantly from the car.

NO TEXT, NO LETTERS, NO WORDS, NO NUMBERS, NO SIGNS, NO LABELS anywhere in the light artwork or anywhere in the photo.

REFERENCE IMAGE 1 governs ONLY the physical construction of the light curtain — wires, bulbs, spacing, see-through quality. Take nothing else from it. REFERENCE IMAGE 2 governs ONLY camera framing and layout.

SURFACE OVERRIDE: wherever the block below says "garage door", read "gable end siding wall of the house" — the curtain hangs on the triangular gable end of the house, not on a garage door. Everything else in the block applies exactly as written.

CRITICAL — HOW THE LIGHTS MUST BE BUILT. The picture on the garage door is NOT an LED video wall, NOT a screen, NOT a projection. It is a hanging curtain of LED string lights, physically identical to REFERENCE IMAGE 1: many thin vertical wires hung side by side from a header cable pinned under the garage lintel, each wire carrying small round bulbs. Build the whole picture out of VERTICAL DOTTED LINES OF LIGHT. The horizontal gap between neighbouring strands is visibly WIDER than the vertical gap between bulbs on the same strand, so the grid is tall and rectangular — never a square pixel matrix.

The curtain is SEE-THROUGH. Through the gaps between strands you clearly read the white garage door behind it: its horizontal panel seams, its rim of trim, the faint shadows of the wires falling on it. The unlit areas are NOT a solid coloured field — they are simply dark garage door, with the unlit bulbs showing as tiny dull grey dots and the thin dark wires as faint vertical lines.

Every lit bulb is a tiny point source: blown-out white core, coloured halo bleeding around it, a small soft star flare, individually countable. The artwork is extremely low resolution and blocky — chunky stepped edges, no smooth curves, no thin outlines, no small facial detail, no gradients, no anti-aliasing.

The curtain hangs a few inches proud of the door: the strands sag slightly, each strand ends at a slightly different height so the bottom hem is uneven and wavy, a faint breeze tilts the sheet, and the glow bounces back off the door behind it as a soft halo. Light spills onto the snow in a speckled uneven pattern, not a flat even wash.

ABSOLUTELY NO: LED video wall, TV screen, monitor, jumbotron, projector image, black backing panel, bezel or frame, dense square pixel grid, solid saturated background field, smooth clean vector artwork. Real amateur photo, natural noise, no CGI polish.
